News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
Winter Norovirus Spread Raises Concern... Seoul to Step Up Infection Prevention at Daycare Centers and Kindergartens - 파이낸셜뉴스
News Brief
The Seoul Metropolitan Government is taking proactive measures to combat the spread of norovirus in daycare centers and kindergartens ahead of the winter season. With norovirus detections rising sharply in summer 2026—about 2.2 times higher than the previous year—preparations are being made to address the virus's seasonal prevalence. The government plans to hold a symposium on November 19, 2026, at Seoul City Hall’s Seosomun Annex, where infectious disease experts and frontline workers will share knowledge on transmission routes, prevention, and management. The event will also cover guidelines for managing food-preparation environments and using disinfectants like sodium hypochlorite. The symposium aims to enhance infection control practices and ensure rapid response to outbreaks, particularly in facilities where infants and young children are concentrated. The government will also distribute prevention guidelines by year-end. Norovirus, a major cause of acute gastroenteritis, typically peaks in winter, with cases increasing as temperatures drop. The Seoul Metropolitan Government emphasized the importance of early intervention to mitigate the risk of widespread infections.

Wednesday, November 18, 2026 한국어 Family Site The financial news navigation all search Finance·Stock Realestate Tech·IT Economy Politics Society World K-wave Opinion Close layer The financial news Finance·Stock Finance Stock Realestate Policy Construction Railway·Air·Ship Real estate-General Tech·IT Industry Communications·Broadcasting Game Internet Blockchain Medicine·Science Economy Economy-General Living Economy Politics President's Office Presidential Office·Party North Korea National-defense Society Incident·Accident Prosecution·Court Administration Education Nationwide World World-economy World-politics World-Society K-wave Taste of Korea K-Style K-Game·eSports K-culture K-Health K-sports Crypto Briefing Hallyu&World trend Opinion Editorial Column Contribution About Us Terms of Service Copyright Policy Privacy Policy Follow us facebook x Close layer search Winter Norovirus Spread Raises Concern... Seoul to Step Up Infection Prevention at Daycare Centers and Kindergartens Input 2026-11-16 15:45:38 Updated 2026-11-16 15:45:38 zoom in zoom out share facebook x URL [Financial News] Ahead of the winter norovirus season, the Seoul Metropolitan Government will step up infection prevention and response systems at daycare centers and kindergartens. Last year, at least half of norovirus infection patients were infants and young children, and detections this summer also rose sharply from a year earlier, increasing the need for infection control at congregate living facilities. The Seoul Metropolitan Government said on the 16th that it will hold the “2026 Norovirus Infection Management Symposium for Daycare Centers and Kindergartens” at 3 p.m. on the 19th in the main conference room at Seoul City Hall’s Seosomun Annex. Norovirus is a major cause of acute gastroenteritis, which can cause vomiting and diarrhea. Patient numbers tend to rise from November as temperatures fall, with outbreaks continuing through January of the following year. Early response is especially important at daycare centers and kindergartens, where infants and young children are concentrated, to prevent the spread of infection. According to sentinel surveillance conducted by the Korea Disease Control and Prevention Agency at 210 hospital-level medical institutions nationwide, 814 norovirus detections were recorded from June to August this year, about 2.2 times the 377 recorded during the same period last year. With detections rising sharply even in summer, when outbreaks are typically less common, preparations for the winter outbreak are needed. At the symposium, infectious disease experts and frontline workers will share the main transmission routes of norovirus and ways to prevent and manage infection. They will also provide guidance on managing food-preparation environments at daycare centers and kindergartens, as well as on the types of disinfectants, including sodium hypochlorite, and their proper use. Cases of infection at daycare centers and the response process will also be presented. In particular, the symposium will explain standard operating procedures for promptly handling bodily fluids such as vomit and disinfecting the environment, and will offer response methods that can be used on site. About 120 people are expected to attend, including daycare center and kindergarten staff, officials from district public health centers, and infectious disease experts. Members of the general public may also participate. The Seoul Metropolitan Government plans to produce video training materials covering the symposium’s key content and post them on the city’s website. It also plans to distribute infection prevention and management guidelines and standard operating procedures to daycare centers and kindergartens by the end of the year. Cho Young-chang, head of the Seoul Metropolitan Government’s Citizen Health Bureau, said, “Norovirus infections are prevalent in winter, and infants and young children account for a high proportion of infections, making advance prevention and a rapid initial response at daycare centers and kindergartens especially important. We will continue our education and public outreach efforts to ensure that the expert knowledge and frontline experience shared at this symposium lead to effective infection prevention and management.” longss@fnnews.com Seong Cho-rong Reporter NEWS Top 10 Was There an Accomplice in ‘AI Tax Accountant Exam Cheating’? Reiterated Economy US Europe Asia Global Economic Calendar Economic Scorecard Crypto Cryptocurrency Blockchain Markets Market News Morning Mkt Analysis US Commentary European Commentary Asian Commentary Canadian Commentary Indian Commentary Commodities Bonds Currencies Politics US World White House Elections General News Forex FX Top Stories Currency Analysis Currency Alerts Economic Calendar Economic Scorecard Health Health News Coronavirus COVID-19 Calendar Diet & Fitness Cannabis Kids Health Men's Health Women's Health Cancer News Drug Development Mental Health Slide Shows Entertainment Entertainment News Top Stories Music News Pop Rock Classic Rock Rap/Hip-Hop Country Alternative Oldies All Genre Content Licensing Newswires & Feeds Content Syndication Digital Signage Services Radio News Services Premium Intelligent Investor Biotech Investor Login More Latest News Videos Free Content RSS Feeds Press Releases Search Contact Us Biotech Daily Dose Cocrystal Q3 Loss Widens On Higher Costs; Norovirus Data Expected Near Year-End By RTTNews Staff Writer ✉ | Published: 11/16/2026 2:18 AM ET | Follow Us | Join Us Add as your preferred news source on Google Add Now Cocrystal Pharma, Inc. (COCP) reported a wider third-quarter loss as spending increased on its norovirus trial and administrative activities. Company Profile Cocrystal is a clinical-stage biotechnology company developing antiviral treatments for norovirus, influenza and other viral infections. Third-quarter Financial Results For the quarter ended September 30, 2026, net loss widened to $3.44 million, or $0.20 per share, from $2.05 million, or $0.19 per share, a year earlier. Research and development expenses increased to $1.98 million from $0.95 million, primarily reflecting costs associated with the Phase 1b CDI-988 norovirus challenge study. General and administrative expenses rose to $1.62 million from $1.13 million, driven by higher salaries, investor and public relations spending, and legal fees, partially offset by lower other administrative costs. Revenue and grant income totaled $0.105 million, compared with none in the prior-year quarter, reflecting funding for its influenza A/B inhibitor program. Cash and restricted cash amounted to $4.95 million as of September 30. Clinical Updates CDI-988 — Norovirus Cocrystal completed dosing in its Phase 1b norovirus challenge study in September, with no serious adverse events reported. Preliminary efficacy results are expected near year-end 2026. The oral protease inhibitor received FDA Fast Track designation in April. CC-42344 — Influenza A The company plans an additional Phase 2a trial of oral CC-42344. Its initial study showed favorable safety and tolerability, but efficacy analyses were not reported because of trial-conduct issues. For the inhaled formulation, Cocrystal has developed a dry powder version and completed toxicology studies. Influenza A/B Program Development of a candidate targeting the influenza A/B polymerase complex is supported by an approximately $500,000 NIH grant awarded in October 2025. Business Updates Cocrystal raised $5 million through a private placement with OPKO Health, selling approximately 5.47 million shares at $0.9134 each. Proceeds will support drug development, working capital and general corporate purposes. The company appointed former Gilead Sciences executive Carol Brosgart to its board, effective August 12, adding antiviral development and regulatory experience. Stock Quote The shares of Cocrystal have traded between $0.76 and $2.03 over the past year. COCP closed Friday trading at $0.98, down 1%. 冬季诺如病毒警告:去年一半以上病例是幼儿

Winter norovirus warning: More than half of last year's cases were toddlers
新闻简报首尔市政府针对随着冬季临近,发病率感染病例不断增加发出警告,强调托儿所和幼儿园需要及早预防并迅速应对。根据 KDCA 监测 数据,去年的 诺如病毒 病例中有一半以上涉及 6 岁及以下儿童,由于免疫系统较弱,他们特别容易受到伤害。该市将于 2026 年 11 月 17 日组织一次研讨会,讨论关键的 传播 因素、正确的消毒方法和现实世界的应对案例。该活动将邀请传染病专家参加,并包括来自成功管理 暴发 的日托中心的最佳实践示例。此外,政府计划根据研讨会制作教育材料,并将其分发给日托和幼儿园工作人员以及公共卫生中心工作人员。研讨会预计将吸引约120名与会者,其中包括教育工作者和官员。该警告强调了 诺如病毒 感染的季节性特征,该感染在 11 月至 1 月达到高峰,并强调了在高风险环境中采取主动措施的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于 2026 年 11 月 17 日。没有明确说明事件日期,但随着冬季临近,诺如病毒 感染从 11 月到 1 月不断增加,发出了警告。
地点与对象
该活动以韩国首尔为中心,针对日托中心和幼儿园。受影响人群包括6岁及以下儿童,他们更容易受到诺如病毒感染。
事件
首尔卫生当局警告称,随着冬季临近,诺如病毒 感染病例可能会增加。首尔市政府正在为日托中心和幼儿园组织一次研讨会,讨论 传播、消毒和应对策略。该事件已根据官方声明得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
根据KDCA 监测数据,去年的诺如病毒案件中有一半以上涉及6岁及以下儿童。夏季 6 月至 8 月,诺如病毒 检测增加至 814 例,是去年同期记录的 377 例的 2.2 倍。由于该病毒的季节性高峰和幼儿的脆弱性,该风险被明确指出为高风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
首尔市政府正在积极组织托儿所和幼儿园研讨会,分享诺如病毒 传播、消毒和应对等信息。他们还计划根据研讨会制作视频教材并发送给相关工作人员。响应已得到确认并正在进行中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Winter norovirus warning: More than half of last year's cases were toddlers

News Brief
The Seoul Metropolitan Government has issued a warning about the rising incidence of norovirus infections as winter approaches, emphasizing the need for early prevention and swift response in daycare centers and kindergartens. According to KDCA surveillance data, more than half of last year's norovirus cases involved children aged 6 and under, who are particularly vulnerable due to weaker immune systems. The city is organizing a symposium on November 17, 2026, to address key transmission factors, proper disinfection methods, and real-world response cases. The event will feature infectious disease experts and include best-practice examples from daycare centers that have successfully managed outbreaks. Additionally, the government plans to produce educational materials based on the symposium and distribute them to daycare and kindergarten staff, as well as public health center personnel. The symposium is expected to attract about 120 participants, including educators and officials. The warning highlights the seasonal nature of norovirus infections, which peak from November through January, and underscores the importance of proactive measures in high-risk environments.

Sign In ECONOMY FINANCE STOCK REAL ESTATE INDUSTRY POLITICS SOCIETY WORLD SMB·BIO IT·SCIENCE CONSUMER LIFE·CULTURE OPINION ENTERTAINMENT SPORTS ECONOMY FINANCE STOCK REAL ESTATE INDUSTRY POLITICS SOCIETY WORLD SMB·BIO IT·SCIENCE CONSUMER LIFE·CULTURE OPINION SOCIETYWinter norovirus warning: More than half of last year's cases were toddlers SOCIETY Winter norovirus warning: More than half of last year's cases were toddlers by Shin Sang-yoon Published : Nov. 17, 2026 - 18:41:27 View Korean Original Link copied! Seoul Metropolitan Government to hold symposium Thursday for daycare centers and kindergartens on transmission, disinfection and response [Provided by Seoul Metropolitan Government] As winter approaches, health authorities are warning of a rise in norovirus infections. Data show that more than one in two patients recorded in sentinel surveillance last year were children aged 6 and under, a group with comparatively weaker immune systems. In response, the Seoul Metropolitan Government is organizing an event for local daycare centers and kindergartens to share information on key transmission factors, proper disinfection methods and real-world response cases. The city announced Tuesday that it will hold the "2026 Norovirus Infection Management Symposium for Daycare Centers and Kindergartens" at 3 p.m. Thursday at the main conference room of Seoul City Hall's Seosomun Annex. Norovirus causes acute gastroenteritis, with symptoms including vomiting and diarrhea. According to KDCA surveillance data, norovirus infections tend to increase from November, when temperatures begin to drop, and remain prevalent through January of the following year. Even during the off-season summer months of June through August, norovirus detections at 210 hospital-level medical institutions nationwide reached 814 cases — roughly 2.2 times the 377 cases recorded during the same period last year. The symposium will focus on practical prevention and management measures. Infectious disease experts will explain the main transmission factors behind norovirus and outline prevention and response strategies, while also providing guidance on kitchen environment management and proper disinfectant use that daycare centers and kindergartens can put into practice. The event will also feature best-practice cases from daycare centers that have successfully managed norovirus outbreaks. About 120 participants are expected, including daycare and kindergarten staff, public health center officials from district offices, and experts from relevant academic societies, associations and the infectious disease field. Members of the general public are also welcome to attend in person. The Seoul Metropolitan Government plans to produce video educational materials based on the symposium's key presentations and post them on the city's official website. The materials will also be used in education and outreach efforts targeting daycare and kindergarten teachers and public health center staff, in coordination with related agencies. "Since norovirus infections peak in winter and a high proportion of cases involve young children, early prevention and swift initial response at daycare centers and kindergartens are of utmost importance," said Jo Yeong-chang, director of the Seoul Metropolitan Government's Public Health Bureau. ken@heraldcorp.com This content was produced with the assistance of AI translation services. by Shin Sang-yoon Sejong Center for the Performing Arts transforms back garden into winter market Winter norovirus warning: More than half of last year's cases were toddlers Seoul's final 'Seoult-ing' matchmaking event set for Dec. 12 at DDP MOST READ 1 Malaysia's BE International brings 1,500-strong incentive tour to Korea, bypassing Turkey Nov. 16, 2026 2 AI singer tops Melon chart for first time with 'Did You Become the Adult You Dreamed Of?' Cocrystal Pharma Completes Dosing in Phase 1b Norovirus Challenge Study of CDI-988, Reports No Serious Adverse Events and $5 Million OPKO Investment - Quiver Quantitative

Cocrystal Pharma Completes Dosing in Phase 1b Norovirus Challenge Study of CDI-988, Reports No Serious Adverse Events and $5 Million OPKO Investment - Quiver Quantitative
新闻简报Cocrystal Pharma has completed dosing in its Phase 1b norovirus challenge study of CDI-988, with no serious adverse events reported. The study, conducted at Emory University School of Medicine, involved 32 healthy adults aged 18 to 49. Preliminary efficacy results are expected by late 2026, which could influence the company's future development and investment prospects. The company also secured $5 million in a private placement with OPKO Health and appointed Dr. Carol Brosgart, an expert in antiviral drug development, to its Board of Directors. However, the company reported a net loss of $3,444,000 for the third quarter of 2026, an increase from the previous year, and higher operating expenses, raising concerns about financial stability. These findings are based on the study's completion and are still under investigation for efficacy confirmation.

1b期研究已完成,预计将于2026年底获得初步疗效结果。该消息发布于2026年11月15日。

该研究在埃默里大学医学院进行,涉及 32 名 18 岁至 49 岁的健康成年人。

Cocrystal Pharma 在 CDI-988 的 1b 期 诺如病毒 挑战研究中完成了给药,报告没有严重不良事件。该研究是旨在开发用于 诺如病毒 预防和 治疗 的 抗病毒药物 疗法的临床计划的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
该研究涉及 32 名健康成年人,没有报告严重不良事件。预计将于 2026 年底获得初步疗效结果,这可能会影响公司未来的发展和投资机会。没有报告具体的卫生系统或运营影响。

该研究正在进行中,预计将于 2026 年末得出初步疗效结果。该公司已报告财务业绩,包括 2026 年第三季度的净亏损和增加的运营费用。该研究的结果尚未得到证实。

Full Release Completed dosing in Phase 1b challenge study of CDI-988 for norovirus prevention and treatment with no serious adverse events reported Preliminary efficacy results expected near the end of 2026 Closed $5 million private placement with OPKO Health Appointed antiviral drug development leader Carol Brosgart, MD, to the Board of Directors BOTHELL, Wash., Nov. 15, 2026 (GLOBE NEWSWIRE) -- Cocrystal Pharma, Inc. (Nasdaq: COCP) (“Cocrystal” or the “Company”) provides updates on its antiviral product pipeline and business activities and reports financial results for the three and nine months ended September 30, 2026. “Dosing the last subject in our Phase 1b challenge study brings Cocrystal to a key value inflection point. With the continued support of OPKO Health and a board strengthened by Dr. Brosgart’s antiviral development experience, we are focused on reporting preliminary efficacy data from CDI-988 during late 2026 and positioning the program for its next stage of clinical development,” said James Sapirstein, Chief Executive Officer of Cocrystal. “With dosing now complete in the Phase 1b challenge study, the preliminary efficacy results will be an important test of what CDI-988 can do. OPKO increased its investment this quarter because norovirus represents both a substantial global need and an equally substantial commercial opportunity, and we believe CDI-988 has the potential to address both,” said Phillip Frost, M.D., Chairman and Chief Executive Officer of OPKO Health and co-founder, director and principal stockholder of Cocrystal. “The study design lets us evaluate CDI-988 in both prevention and treatment settings, and completing dosing is an important step for the program. With no approved treatments or vaccines for norovirus, we look forward to efficacy data that will guide CDI-988’s path toward later-stage development,” said Sam Lee, Ph.D., President and Chief Scientific Officer of Cocrystal. The Phase 1b randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled challenge study (NCT07198139) is being conducted at Emory University School of Medicine. The study enrolled 32 healthy adults, aged 18 to 49, in staged cohorts. Following a stage 1 infectivity cohort, subjects in the prevention and treatment cohorts were inoculated with the norovirus GII.2 (Snow Mountain Virus) strain and received CDI-988 at 1,200 mg twice daily for five days or placebo. The last subject was dosed in September 2026, with no serious adverse events reported. The primary efficacy endpoint is reduction in the incidence of clinical symptoms versus placebo, with secondary endpoints including reduction in viral shedding, disease severity, safety and pharmacokinetics. Preliminary efficacy results are expected in late 2026. Antiviral Product Pipeline Overview We leverage our proprietary structure-based drug discovery platform technology to develop next-generation, broad-spectrum antivirals that effectively block viral replication. Unlike other drug discovery approaches, our technology identifies compounds that bind to highly conserved regions of viral drug targets, including proteases and replication enzymes. By specifically targeting these essential viral functions, our drug candidates maintain efficacy as viruses mutate, while simultaneously minimizing off-target interactions that may lead to adverse side effects. This dual advantage represents a significant breakthrough in antiviral drug development. In addition, our innovative methodology fundamentally transforms the conventional drug discovery paradigm by eliminating inefficient, resource-intensive cycles of high-throughput compound screening and prolonged hit-to-lead optimization. The result is faster identification of promising candidates with superior resistance and safety characteristics. Norovirus Program Norovirus is a common, highly contagious virus that afflicts people of all ages and causes symptoms of acute gastroenteritis: nausea, vomiting, stomach pain and diarrhea, fatigue, fever and dehydration. There are currently no effective treatments or vaccines for norovirus, and the ability to curtail outbreaks is inadequate. With 685 million global cases annually and a $60 billion worldwide economic impact, norovirus represents one of healthcare's most pressing unmet needs. In the U.S., noroviruses are responsible for an estimated 21 million infections annually, including an estimated 109,000 hospitalizations, 465,000 emergency department visits and 900 deaths. The annual burden of norovirus to the U.S. is estimated at $10.6 billion. In the developing world, each year noroviruses are responsible for up to 1.1 million hospitalizations and 218,000 pediatric deaths. Oral protease inhibitor CDI-988 for the treatment of noroviruses and coronaviruses: Our first oral direct-acting antiviral CDI-988 targets the highly conserved region of the 3CL protease and is designed as a potential therapeutic for noroviruses and coronaviruses. CDI-988 has shown in vitro activity against multiple norovirus strains. In April 2025 we announced that CDI-988 showed superior broad-spectrum antiviral activity against the norovirus GII.17 strain, the most prevalent strain in the U.S. and Europe in 2024-2025. In August 2025 we presented favorable Phase 1 safety and tolerability data from all CDI-988 doses, including a high-dose 1,200 mg cohort, at the 2025 Military Health System Research Symposium (MHSRS). In September 2025 we discussed CDI-988's scientific foundation and clinical progress in an oral presentation at the 9th International Calicivirus Conference, the leading calicivirus scientific meeting. In September 2025 we received a Study May Proceed Letter from the FDA to conduct a Phase 1b challenge study in the U.S. evaluating CDI-988 as a norovirus preventive and treatment. In March 2026 we enrolled the first subjects in our Phase 1b challenge study, which is being conducted at Emory University School of Medicine. In April 2026 we announced full enrollment in the first cohort of the Phase 1b study, which is evaluating the infectivity rate of the GII.2 challenge inoculum, at the International Conference on Antiviral Research 2026 (ICAR 2026). In April 2026 we were granted FDA Fast Track designation for CDI-988, enabling the potential for an accelerated development pathway. In September 2026 we dosed the last subject in the Phase 1b challenge study, with no serious adverse events reported to date. We expect to report preliminary efficacy results from the Phase 1b study near the end of 2026 Influenza Programs Influenza is a major global health threat that may become more challenging to treat due to the emergence of highly pathogenic avian influenza viruses and resistance to approved influenza antivirals. Currently approved antiviral treatments for influenza are effective but are burdened with significant viral resistance. Each year approximately 1 billion cases of seasonal influenza, 3-5 million severe illnesses and up to 650,000 deaths are reported worldwide. About 8% of the U.S. population gets sick from flu each season. In addition to the health risk, influenza is responsible for an estimated $10.4 billion in direct medical costs in the U.S. each year. CC-42344 is our novel PB2 inhibitor that showed excellent in vitro activity against pandemic and seasonal influenza A strains, as well as against strains that are resistant to Tamiflu ® and Xofluza ® . Oral CC-42344 as a treatment for pandemic and seasonal influenza A In December 2022 we reported favorable Phase 1 safety and tolerability results. In December 2023 we began a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled Phase 2a human challenge study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and viral and clinical measurements of CC-42344 in influenza A-infected subjects in the United Kingdom, following authorization from the UK Medicines and Healthcare Products Regulatory Agency. In May 2025 we reported that CC-42344 was shown to be active against the highly pathogenic 2024 Texas H5N1 avian influenza strain. In November 2025 an initial Phase 2a study was completed, with CC-42344 showing a favorable safety and tolerability profile with no serious adverse events and no drug-related discontinuations by study participants. Efficacy analyses were not reported due to issues with trial conduct. We plan to continue development of oral CC-42344 as a treatment for pandemic and seasonal influenza A with an additional Phase 2a study. Inhaled CC-42344 as prophylaxis and treatment for pandemic and seasonal influenza A Our preclinical testing showed superior pulmonary pharmacology with CC-42344, including high exposure to drug and a long half-life. We have developed a dry powder inhalation formulation of CC-42344 and have completed toxicology studies. Influenza A/B program In October 2025 we were awarded an approximately $500,000 Small Business Innovation Research (SBIR) Phase I grant from the National Institutes of Health's (NIH) National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases to support the development of a novel, broad-spectrum lead candidate targeting the influenza A/B polymerase complex. Recent Corporate Developments On July 31, 2026, the Company entered into a Securities Purchase Agreement with longtime investor OPKO Health, Inc. (Nasdaq: OPK), under which the Company sold 5,474,053 shares of common stock at $0.9134 per share, the Nasdaq Consolidated Bid Price on the trading day of closing, for gross proceeds of $5.0 million. The Company intends to use the net proceeds for the advancement of its preclinical and clinical drug candidate programs, and for general corporate purposes and working capital. In August 2026 the Company appointed Carol Brosgart, MD, to its Board of Directors, effective August 12, 2026. Dr. Brosgart spent more than a decade at Gilead Sciences, where she played a critical role in the development and regulatory approval of the antiviral therapies Viread ® and Hepsera ® . She currently serves as Clinical Professor of Medicine, Biostatistics and Epidemiology at the University of California, San Francisco (UCSF) School of Medicine. 冬季病来袭,诺如病毒 两周内增加 40%,孩子们出现呕吐和腹泻症状

Winter sickness strikes as norovirus up 40% in two weeks and kids struck by vomiting and diarrhoea bug
新闻简报Norovirus cases in the UK rose by 40.4 per cent in two weeks, according to the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA). This increase is within expected levels for the winter season, which typically sees a surge in norovirus infections as colder weather encourages indoor socializing. The virus, known as the winter vomiting bug, primarily affects babies and young children, causing severe symptoms like vomiting and diarrhoea. UKHSA reported 72 rotavirus cases in the two weeks leading up to November 8, which was 25 per cent higher than the usual average for this time of year. While norovirus cases were up, they remained 9 per cent below the typical early November average. The agency urged people to maintain good hand hygiene, avoid contaminated food, and stay home for 48 hours after symptoms subside. Vulnerable groups, including babies, young children, older people, and those with weakened immune systems, are at higher risk of severe dehydration and require medical attention if symptoms persist or worsen.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年10月26日至11月8日期间,发布日期2026年11月12日。
地点与对象
英国,主要影响婴儿和幼儿。
事件
Norovirus cases increased by 40.4 per cent in two weeks, as reported by the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA).
规模、影响与风险
72 rotavirus cases were recorded in the two weeks leading up to November 8, which was 25 per cent higher than the average for this time of year. Norovirus cases were 40.4 per cent higher than the previous two weeks but still 9 per cent lower than the typical early November average. Symptoms include vomiting, diarrhoea, fever, and dehydration, which can be severe in vulnerable groups.
应对、状态与不确定性
UKHSA 报告称,诺如病毒 病例激增,符合预期水平。该机构强调了清洁和消毒受污染表面以及保持良好手部卫生的重要性。提供了有关何时寻求医疗帮助的指导,包括严重脱水的迹象和长期症状。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Winter sickness strikes as norovirus up 40% in two weeks and kids struck by vomiting and diarrhoea bug

News Brief
Norovirus cases in the UK rose by 40.4 per cent in two weeks, according to the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA). This increase is within expected levels for the winter season, which typically sees a surge in norovirus infections as colder weather encourages indoor socializing. The virus, known as the winter vomiting bug, primarily affects babies and young children, causing severe symptoms like vomiting and diarrhoea. UKHSA reported 72 rotavirus cases in the two weeks leading up to November 8, which was 25 per cent higher than the usual average for this time of year. While norovirus cases were up, they remained 9 per cent below the typical early November average. The agency urged people to maintain good hand hygiene, avoid contaminated food, and stay home for 48 hours after symptoms subside. Vulnerable groups, including babies, young children, older people, and those with weakened immune systems, are at higher risk of severe dehydration and require medical attention if symptoms persist or worsen.
Original source text (English)
NOROVIRUS cases have shot up by 40 per cent in two weeks, though the increase is expected, health chiefs say. Meanwhile, another vomiting and diarrhoea bug that primarily strikes kids has surged, with cases 25 per cent higher than what’s usually seen at this time of year. Cases of norovirus have risen 40 per cent in two weeks Source: UKHSA Known as rotavirus , the infection is very common in babies and young children and spreads easily. It can cause severe, watery diarrhoea, vomiting, fever and loss of appetite. Sign up for the Health newsletter Thank you! Babies are usually vaccinated against it, as they may require hospital treatment for dangerous dehydration if the infection is severe. The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) said 72 rotavirus cases were recorded in the two weeks leading up to November 8. Cases of rotavirus are above the average seen at this time of year Source: UKHSA Rotavirus tends to affect babies and young children Source: Getty This was nearly 6 per cent higher than the 68 positive laboratory tests recorded in the previous two week period, and 25 per cent higher than the average number of cases typically seen at this time of year. More norovirus cases were reported, though these remained within expected levels, according to UKHSA. There were 257 positive lab tests indicating norovirus between October 26 and November 8. This was 40.4 per cent higher than the 183 cases recorded during the previous two weeks. But it was still almost 9 per cent lower than the average of 282 cases typically seen in early November. Norovirus is known as the winter vomiting bug as it tends to surge as the weather gets colder, peaking in December or January. That’s because colder temperatures encourage people to socialise indoors, creating the perfect conditions for the virus to spread. The typical symptoms of norovirus include sudden onset diarrhoea and projectile vomiting. People may also experience stomach cramps, a headache, aching limbs and, in some cases, a mild fever. Symptoms last about two or three days and can be treated at home with rest and drinking plenty of fluids. But some people might need medical treatment if they get severely dehydrated or they’re more at risk from severe illness. This includes babies, young children, older people and those with compromised immune systems. How to treat D&V bugs and when to call 999 If you’ve got norovirus: Stay at home and rest Do not return to work or send children to school until 48 hours after the symptoms have stopped Don’t visit your GP or hospital while symptomatic – if you’re concerned about your symptoms, talk to your GP by phone, contact NHS 111 or visit the NHS norovirus webpage Drink plenty of fluids to prevent dehydration from vomiting and diarrhoea Avoid cooking and helping to prepare meals for others until 48 hours after symptoms have stopped, as norovirus can be spread through contaminated food It is important to wash hands regularly and clean, wash or disinfect thoroughly any contaminated items or surfaces such as clothes, bedding, toilets, taps and door handles You might need to speak to a doctor if: You’re worried about your baby, who is younger than 12 months old. Your child stops breast or bottle feeding while they’re ill You think your child under five might be dehydrated – signs could include fewer wet nappies. You’ve used rehydration sachets but still have signs of dehydration You or your child keep being sick and can’t keep fluids down. You or your child have bloody diarrhoea or start bleeding from the bottom. You or your child have diarrhoea for more than seven days or are vomiting for more than two days. And if you spot any of the following signs, it’s vital to seek urgent care: Blood in your vomit Vomit that looks like ground coffee Green vomit (adults) or yellow-green vomit (children) A stiff neck Pain when looking at bright lights A sudden, severe headache or stomach ache Source: UKHSA and NHS How to protect yourself against winter vomiting bugs Norovirus and rotavirus both spread easily through close contact, contaminated surfaces and contaminated food. The culprit is typically poo particles containing the bugs. You may ingest the viruses after touching surfaces or objects that have the bug on them and then touching your mouth. The same can happen if you eat food prepared by someone infected. Outbreaks of norovirus are particularly common in spaces where people remain in close contact with one another for long periods of time, such as hospitals, care homes , schools and cruise ships. Norovirus can survive on surfaces for days or weeks, which is why the virus spreads so easily through communities. That’s why it’s important to properly clean and disinfect contaminated surfaces using a bleach-based solution and wearing disposable gloves where possible. And as vomiting bugs become more common this winter, making sure you and your family maintain good hand hygiene is very important. This means washing your hands frequently and thoroughly with soap and warm water, particularly after using the toilet, before eating or preparing food, and after cleaning up vomit or diarrhoea. Take note: alcohol-based hand sanitisers don’t kill norovirus. As for rotavirus, protect your child by making sure they get the protective vaccine. Babies are given two doses of the rotavirus vaccine as part of the NHS vaccination schedule when they are eight and 12 weeks old. If your baby has missed their rotavirus vaccinations, contact their GP surgery

Milton elementary school to close, norovirus outbreak suspected - WKOW

News Brief
On November 12, 2026, the Milton elementary school in Wisconsin was reported to be closing due to a suspected norovirus outbreak. The outbreak was identified as a potential health concern, prompting the closure of the school to prevent further spread. The evidence indicates that the school's closure is a precautionary measure, but no specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers were provided. The situation is currently under investigation, and the response status remains uncertain. The norovirus outbreak is suspected, but the scale and impact of the outbreak are not detailed in the supplied information. The school's closure is a direct response to the suspected outbreak, highlighting the potential risk to students and staff.

上班那天有人呕吐怎么办?职业医师解释如何应对 诺如病毒 暴发

What to do on the day someone vomits at work | An occupational physician explains how to respond to a norovirus outbreak
新闻简报这篇发表于 2026 年 11 月 15 日的文章讨论了如何在工作场所应对 诺如病毒 暴发,正如一位职业医生的解释。它强调了 诺如病毒 和流感之间的主要区别,例如酒精消毒无效以及需要适当的次氯酸钠溶液。该指南包括将受影响的人员送回家,指定专人进行清理,并确保对受污染的表面进行彻底消毒。它还强调了监测其他病例并在怀疑食物中毒时咨询公共卫生中心的重要性。该文章没有报告具体病例或死亡人数,但概述了 诺如病毒 通过呕吐物和粪便传播的风险,即使在症状消退后,呕吐物和粪便仍可能具有传染性。建议采取的行动重点是防止进一步的 传播 并有效管理局势。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
工作场所、未指定地点、一般人群。
事件
在工作场所报告了 诺如病毒 暴发,职业医生提供了如何应对此类事件的指导。
规模、影响与风险
没有报告具体病例或死亡人数。由于该病毒对酒精具有抵抗力并且能够通过受污染的表面和气溶胶传播,因此该风险与在工作场所快速传播的可能性有关。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文概述了处理 诺如病毒 暴发 的建议行动,包括将受影响的个人送回家、指定清理人员、适当的消毒程序以及通知相关部门。它还提到,在疑似食物中毒等特殊情况下,需要向公共卫生中心咨询。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

What to do on the day someone vomits at work | An occupational physician explains how to respond to a norovirus outbreak

News Brief
The article, published on November 15, 2026, discusses how to respond to a norovirus outbreak in the workplace, as explained by an occupational physician. It highlights key differences between norovirus and influenza, such as the ineffectiveness of alcohol-based disinfection and the need for proper sodium hypochlorite solutions. The guidance includes sending affected individuals home, designating specific personnel for cleanup, and ensuring thorough disinfection of contaminated surfaces. It also emphasizes the importance of monitoring for additional cases and consulting the public health center if food poisoning is suspected. The article does not report specific case or death counts but outlines the risks of norovirus spreading through vomit and stool, which can remain infectious even after symptoms subside. The recommended actions focus on preventing further transmission and managing the situation effectively.

メインコンテンツへスキップ SYSTEM NOTICE Auto translation by AI. Be sure, accuracy, nuances and authorial intent may not be fully reflected. Show original What to do on the day someone vomits at work | An occupational physician explains how to respond to a norovirus outbreak かつ先生の健康手帳|産業医・労働衛生コンサルタント Nov 15, 2026 21:30 On a winter morning, when you arrive at the office, you might hear, "So-and-so vomited in the restroom..." or "It happened right next to their desk..." Someone frantically grabs an alcohol spray, and people nearby start helping to wipe it up with their bare hands—this is a common scene when a situation that looks like norovirus occurs in the workplace. However, norovirus cannot be prevented with the same measures used for influenza. This is because there is a clear difference between disinfectants that are effective and those that are not. In this article, I will outline what HR, general affairs, and health managers should do on the day someone vomits or has diarrhea at work, from the perspective of an occupational physician. First, I will mention three points that differ from influenza. First: Alcohol disinfection is less effective. Norovirus is a type of virus that is resistant to alcohol. Methods considered to completely inactivate it include disinfection with sodium hypochlorite or heat. The Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare also clearly states that alcohol disinfection for hands "is not a substitute for handwashing, but rather a supplement." Second: It spreads in small amounts and is shed in large quantities in vomit and stool. The vomit and stool of an infected person contain a large amount of the virus. Furthermore, dried vomit can become dust containing the virus, which can float in the air and be inhaled. "Thinking you wiped it up" is the most common pattern for spreading it. Third: There is no fixed rule for "how many days to stay home." There is no statutory leave period like there is for influenza. Instead, even after symptoms subside, the virus can continue to be shed in stool for about a week, or up to a month in some cases. Unlike influenza, the criteria for returning to work are based not on "number of days," but on "symptoms and subsequent handwashing." 1. Five things to do on that day (1) Send the person home or have them rest Not forcing someone with vomiting or diarrhea to come to work, or having them leave the area, is the best way to prevent the spread. Symptoms usually subside in 1 to 2 days, but attention must be paid to dehydration. If they cannot drink fluids or symptoms are severe, please recommend they see a medical institution. (2) Decide who will handle the cleanup and the scope People nearby should leave the area, and the cleanup should be handled by a designated person or a small group. Start ventilation, such as opening windows, at this time as well. "Everyone helping" only increases the number of people who come into contact with it. (3) Clean up the vomit correctly The procedure is summarized in "2" below. The key points are three: "Quietly, before it dries, and seal the items you touched for disposal." (4) Disinfect areas that are touched by hands This includes toilet seats, flush levers, doorknobs, handrails, and faucets. The concentration of disinfectant is different from that used for cleaning up vomit. How to prepare it is summarized in "3" below. (5) Notify the department and start a list Inform the department that "if you feel unwell, do not force yourself to come to work" and "the contact person is so-and-so." At the same time, start a list of people who have developed symptoms (date of onset, symptoms, last day at work). Since the incubation period is 24 to 48 hours, this is to monitor the situation on the assumption that the next person will develop symptoms the next day or the day after. 2. Procedure for cleaning up vomit Items to prepare Disposable masks, gloves (double if possible), aprons (gowns), two plastic bags, paper towels, etc., and sodium hypochlorite disinfectant. If you prepare a set as a cleanup kit before winter, you won't panic on the day it happens. Procedure Have people nearby move away and ventilate the area. The person cleaning up should wear a mask, gloves, and an apron. Gently cover the vomit with paper towels or similar material. Gently wipe from the outside toward the center (avoid wiping back and forth, as this spreads the virus). Seal the used paper towels and gloves in a plastic bag and dispose of them. Cover the floor with paper towels soaked in disinfectant, wipe it up, and then wipe with water. Finally, wash your hands with soap and running water. Because viruses can drift into the air from dried vomit, it is important to clean it up promptly before it dries. 3. How to make disinfectant (you only need to remember two concentrations) The concentration of sodium hypochlorite (you can substitute household chlorine bleach) comes in two levels depending on the use. 0.1% (1,000ppm): For treating vomit and feces. Use this for sealing and disposing of items used for wiping, or for areas with heavy contamination. If the product has a 5% concentration, use 20mL of the undiluted solution per 1 liter of water. 0.02% (200ppm): For wiping down the environment. Use this for places touched by hands, such as doorknobs, handrails, and around toilets. If the product has a 5% concentration, use 4mL of the undiluted solution per 1 liter of water. Please also decide on precautions for when you use it. Do not use on hands or fingers. Wash hands with soap and running water. Sodium hypochlorite can damage the skin. Do not make it in advance. Its effectiveness decreases over time, so dilute it only when you are ready to use it. Do not mix with acidic products. Toxic gas may be released. Also, avoid pouring the undiluted solution directly onto vomit. Wipe metal surfaces with water. Because it is corrosive, wipe it off with water about 10 minutes after disinfecting. Be careful of color fading on fabric products. 4. Things you should not do, and things that are counterproductive (1) Relying only on alcohol disinfection Do not assume you have taken measures just by using the alcohol spray at the entrance. Hand washing is the foundation, and sodium hypochlorite should be used for disinfecting objects. (2) Wiping with bare hands or regular rags If you wash and reuse the rags used for cleaning, you will contaminate the entire washing area. The principle is to use disposable items for wiping and seal them in bags for disposal. (3) Putting soiled clothing directly into the washing machine Gently remove the waste, then gently hand-wash in water with detergent. Disinfection is effective through hot water washing at 85°C or higher for at least 1 minute, or by using chlorine-based disinfectants. (4) Rushing to test everyone Norovirus testing is usually judged comprehensively based on symptoms and the surrounding outbreak situation; it is not meant to be used to confirm that everyone is negative. Prioritize letting symptomatic people rest, cleaning up, and hand washing. 5. When to consult the public health center There is no rule requiring general offices to report norovirus outbreaks to the public health center. This is the same as with influenza. However, please consult your local public health center in the following cases: When food poisoning is suspected, such as when multiple people who ate the same meal (school lunch, catering, banquet, etc.) develop symptoms. For workplaces that handle food, such as restaurants and food manufacturing facilities For social welfare facilities such as elderly care facilities and childcare facilities (there are reporting standards for outbreaks) If you are unsure, consulting early makes it easier for the public health center to take action. 6. The week following the incident and guidelines for returning to work Response to an outbreak does not end on the day it occurs. Since the incubation period is 24 to 48 hours, assume that more cases will appear the following day or later; update the list every morning to see if the number of cases is increasing or settling down. Continue disinfecting shared toilets and doorknobs during this period as well. Guidelines for returning to work. To reiterate, there is no legally mandated number of days. In practice, use "after vomiting and diarrhea have subsided and you are able to eat normally" as a guideline. Furthermore, for a while after returning to work (at least about one week), thoroughly wash your hands after using the toilet and avoid sharing towels. Jobs that involve handling food directly (cooking, serving, etc.) are treated differently. Because the virus continues to be shed in stool even after symptoms have subsided, keep individuals away from tasks that involve handling food directly for a while after they have recovered. Summary There are three key points for responding to norovirus. Hand washing: Soap and running water are the basics. Alcohol is supplementary Vomit cleanup: Clean quietly, before it dries, and dispose of it in a sealed container. Use chlorine-based disinfectant at two different concentrations Visualization: Update the list of affected individuals every morning and monitor the spread for one week The peak of the epidemic is usually from December to January. Even just sharing how to make cleanup kits and disinfectant solutions at the workplace before an outbreak occurs will completely change how you handle the situation on the day it happens. How an organization should act when an infectious disease spreads in the workplace is also summarized in the article on responding to influenza outbreaks. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

随着冬季呕吐虫病例增加,UKHSA 诺如病毒“呆在家里”警告 - Chronicle Live

UKHSA norovirus 'stay at home' warning as winter vomiting bug cases rise - Chronicle Live
新闻简报英国健康安全局(UKHSA)已针对诺如病毒发出“呆在家里”警告,敦促有症状的个人在症状消退后留在室内48小时,以防止病毒传播。该指南是遏制冬季呕吐病努力的一部分,与同期五个季节的平均水平相比,冬季呕吐病例增加了 16.8%,迄今为止已收到 2,111 份实验室报告。该病毒通过直接接触和受污染的表面迅速传播,NHS 指出,症状通常在接触后 12 至 48 小时内出现,持续约两到三天。UKHSA 强调,洗手液对 诺如病毒 无效,个人应避免前往学校、托儿所、工作场所、医院和疗养院等公共场所,直到不再有症状。该警告基于已确认的报告和官方指导,没有硬排除或未解决信息的证据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
UKHSA 报告称,本季 诺如病毒 病例有所增加,迄今为止已收到 2,111 份实验室报告,表明与同期五个季节平均水平相比增加了 16.8%。截至上周的两周内,已有 257 份报告注意到了这种传播。文章发表于2026年11月14日。
地点与对象
该警告由英国健康安全局 (UKHSA) 发布,适用于英国普通人群,特别是学校、托儿所、工作场所、医院和疗养院等场所的人群。
事件
UKHSA 已针对 诺如病毒 发出“呆在家里”警告,敦促有症状的个人在症状消退后留在室内 48 小时。这是他们防止病毒传播的指导方针的一部分,这种病毒具有高度传染性,会导致胃和肠道突然发炎。
规模、影响与风险
与同期五个季节的平均水平相比,诺如病毒 病例增加了 16.8%,迄今为止已有 2,111 份实验室报告。该病毒传播迅速,英国卫生安全局警告称,洗手液无法有效预防该病毒。症状通常在接触后 12 至 48 小时内出现,并持续约两至三天。
应对、状态与不确定性
UKHSA 已发布指南,要求有症状的个人呆在家里 48 小时并避免公共场所。NHS 还提供有关管理症状和预防 传播 的建议。该回应基于卫生机构已确认的报告和指导。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

UKHSA norovirus 'stay at home' warning as winter vomiting bug cases rise - Chronicle Live

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) has issued a 'stay at home' warning for norovirus, urging individuals with symptoms to remain indoors for 48 hours after they subside to prevent the spread of the virus. This guidance is part of an effort to curb the winter vomiting bug, which has seen a 16.8% increase in cases compared to the five-season average for the same period, with 2,111 laboratory reports so far. The virus spreads rapidly through direct contact and contaminated surfaces, and the NHS notes that symptoms typically emerge 12 to 48 hours after exposure and last about two to three days. The UKHSA emphasizes that hand sanitiser is ineffective against norovirus, and individuals should avoid public settings like schools, nurseries, workplaces, hospitals, and care homes until they are no longer symptomatic. The warning is based on confirmed reports and official guidance, with no evidence of hard exclusions or unresolved information.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

诺如病毒 关闭威斯康星州米尔顿的综合小学 - Hoodline

Norovirus Closes Consolidated Elementary in Milton, Wisconsin - Hoodline
新闻简报威斯康星州米尔顿市综合小学的 86 名学生中,有超过 35% 的学生因 诺如病毒 暴发 生病,导致学校在本周剩余时间关闭。怀疑是 暴发,关闭的决定是根据罗克县卫生局和学区护士的健康指导做出的。截至 2026 年 11 月 10 日星期三,已有 30 多名学生缺席,而且人数还在持续增加。关闭的目的是确保学生安全并防止进一步传播。官员们计划在 2026 年 11 月 14 日星期一重新开放之前监控其他学校和 Consolidated 学校的缺勤情况。该学校的电话线路受到附近施工的影响,因此通讯被转接到地区办公室。暴发 事件并不是独一无二的,其他学校也发生过类似事件,例如马萨诸塞州梅德福的罗伯茨小学和堪萨斯州欧弗兰帕克的本特伍德小学。然而,证据并未证实具体的病例数或所涉及的具体 诺如病毒 变体。
查看新闻五要素
时间
学校于 2026 年 11 月 12 日宣布停课,暴发 影响整个星期的学生。确切的活动日期尚未确定,但据报告,症状将在 2026 年 11 月 10 日星期三之前出现,并且关闭有效期至 2026 年 11 月 14 日星期一。
地点与对象
该事件发生在威斯康星州米尔顿市的综合小学,影响了 86 名学生。学校为从幼儿园到三年级的学生提供服务。
事件
诺如病毒 导致 Consolidated Elementary 超过 35% 的学生患病,导致学校本周剩余时间关闭。怀疑是 暴发,并根据罗克县卫生部门和学区护士的健康指导而关闭。
规模、影响与风险
86 名学生中超过 35% 受到影响,截至周三已有 30 多人缺席。缺席的确切人数尚未得到确认。暴发 导致学校关闭一周,影响了学生的安全和运营。没有明确说明风险,但关闭表明采取了预防措施,以防止进一步传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
在关闭学校之前,学区咨询了护士和罗克县卫生局。官员们计划在周一重新开放之前监测其他学校和综合学校报告的缺勤情况。关闭是基于健康指导,受影响学生的确切人数尚未得到确认。学校的电话线路受到附近施工的影响,因此通讯被改接到地区办公室。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Norovirus Closes Consolidated Elementary in Milton, Wisconsin - Hoodline

News Brief
A norovirus outbreak sickened over 35% of the 86 students at Consolidated Elementary in Milton, Wisconsin, prompting the school's closure for the rest of the week. The outbreak was suspected, and the decision to close was based on health guidance from the Rock County Health Department and the district's nurse. By Wednesday, November 10, 2026, more than 30 students were absent, with the number continuing to rise. The closure was intended to ensure student safety and prevent further spread. Officials plan to monitor absences at other schools and at Consolidated ahead of its reopening on Monday, November 14, 2026. The school's phone line was affected by nearby construction, so communication was redirected to the district office. The outbreak is not unique, as similar incidents have occurred at other schools, such as Roberts Elementary in Medford, Massachusetts, and Bentwood Elementary in Overland Park, Kansas. However, the evidence does not confirm the exact number of cases or the specific norovirus variant involved.
Original source text (English)

The shutdown leaves the district's smallest school, which serves kindergarten through third grade, dark until Monday, Nov. 16. Thirty students were already absent at the start of Wednesday because of the illness, and that number kept climbing as the day went on, according to Jeremy Bilhorn, who spoke with hngnews.com. More students became sick throughout the day and were sent home, and Bilhorn said he could not confirm the exact end-of-day absence total. “We noticed the numbers rose pretty high today,” he said. District Weighed Health Guidance Before Closing Before deciding to shut the building down, the district consulted its own nurse, Melissa Forrett, along with the Rock County Health Department, the same report notes. Bilhorn said the closure considered student safety and guidance from the county health department, and the district said no other schools in Milton had been affected by the outbreak. Officials plan to monitor reported absences at other schools, as well as at Consolidated ahead of Monday's reopening. Norovirus, often called the stomach flu, spreads through direct contact, food, or water, and typically causes vomiting, watery diarrhea, nausea and stomach cramps. Symptoms usually show up 12 to 48 hours after exposure and last one to three days, per the same account. The district also urged parents and others trying to reach the school to call the district office instead, since nearby construction had caused an outage affecting Consolidated's own phone line. Wastewater Data Offers an Unclear Local Signal Wastewater Data Offers an Unclear Local Signal Wisconsin's health agency keeps a dashboard tracking norovirus levels in wastewater statewide, and Janesville is the only Rock County site that reports into it. A Janesville sample collected Oct. 24 came back rated low for norovirus, according to Wisconsin DHS. The Wisconsin Department of Health Services describes the broader Wastewater Monitoring Program as testing untreated wastewater in communities across the state to track viruses that cause illness, in partnership with the Wisconsin State Lab of Hygiene, the Milwaukee Health Department Lab, and the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee. Other School Outbreaks Show the Pattern Isn't Unique Milton's closure echoes a similar incident reported in December 2025 at an elementary school elsewhere. Roberts Elementary School in Medford, Massachusetts, was scheduled to close after more than 130 students were absent in a single day amid a suspected norovirus outbreak, according to Boston 25 News. There, school officials consulted the Medford Board of Health and the Massachusetts Department of Public Health, who determined the illness was likely norovirus, and a professional cleaning crew was brought in to deep-clean the building while students and staff stayed away. A similar outbreak hit Bentwood Elementary in Overland Park, Kansas, where the case count eventually grew to 97, with 95 of those among students and staff and two more among family members outside the school, as reported by Rockford News First. Officials there said two cases were confirmed through lab testing. The Olathe School District had alerted the county health department once Bentwood absences tied to similar symptoms hit a 10% threshold, according to KCTV5. Nationally, roughly 2,500 norovirus outbreaks are reported each year, a figure health officials believe likely undercounts the true scope of the illness, per Rockford News First's reporting. For Milton families, the immediate question is simpler: whether Consolidated Elementary's halls will be clear of illness by Monday. The district has said it will keep watching absence numbers at its other schools in the meantime, with no confirmed timeline beyond the planned Nov. 16 reopening. 我丈夫被抓住了诺如病毒!!战斗记录!!

My Husband Caught Norovirus!! A Record of the Battle!!
新闻简报这篇个人叙述详细介绍了一位日本妇女的经历,她的丈夫在 2026 年 11 月的周日突然出现症状后被诊断出感染病毒 急性胃肠炎,疑似 诺如病毒。丈夫在午夜左右开始呕吐,到了第二天早上,妻子不得不带他去医院。虽然没有进行具体检查,但医生根据症状建议诺如病毒。随后,妻子采取了广泛的消毒措施,包括使用次氯酸喷雾和沸水来清洁房屋,防止进一步传播。周二,丈夫的症状消退,并于下周重返工作岗位。妻子指出,感染很可能是在周六购物时在公共卫生间发生的,这凸显了洗手和讲卫生对于预防 诺如病毒 传播 的重要性。这次经历强调了在家庭环境中管理 诺如病毒 的挑战以及在公共场所保持警惕的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生在周日,刚刚进入 2026 年 11 月,症状在午夜左右开始,医院就诊于周一中午左右进行。周二早上,病情痊愈,丈夫在下周重返工作岗位。
地点与对象
该事件发生在日本的一个私人家庭,涉及夫妻双方,没有提及具体的社区或设施。受影响人群是丈夫,妻子是主要照顾者。
事件
丈夫突然出现感冒、呕吐、腹泻等症状,被诊断为病毒性急性胃肠炎,疑似诺如病毒。诊断是由一家医院制作的,但没有进行具体测试来确认该病毒。
规模、影响与风险
该事件涉及一例疑似 诺如病毒 感染病例,没有死亡或暴露报告。由于妻子仍然没有症状,因此该家庭的风险被认为较低,但注意到了家庭 传播 的可能性。
应对、状态与不确定性
妻子使用次氯酸喷雾和开水进行消毒,重点对丈夫接触过的区域进行消毒。周二早上,丈夫的症状得到缓解,并于下周重返工作岗位。诊断 基于症状和临床判断,没有经过证实的测试。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

My Husband Caught Norovirus!! A Record of the Battle!!

News Brief
This personal account details the experience of a Japanese woman whose husband was diagnosed with viral acute gastroenteritis, suspected to be norovirus, after a sudden onset of symptoms on Sunday in November 2026. The husband began vomiting around midnight, and by the next morning, the wife had to take him to the hospital. Although no specific test was conducted, the doctor suggested norovirus based on symptoms. The wife then took extensive disinfection measures, including using hypochlorous acid spray and boiling water, to clean the home and prevent further spread. The husband's symptoms subsided by Tuesday, and he returned to work the following week. The wife noted that the infection likely occurred at a public restroom during a shopping trip on Saturday, highlighting the importance of handwashing and hygiene in preventing norovirus transmission. The experience underscored the challenges of managing norovirus in a household setting and the need for vigilance in public spaces.

メインコンテンツへスキップ SYSTEM NOTICE Auto translation by AI. Be sure, accuracy, nuances and authorial intent may not be fully reflected. Show original My Husband Caught Norovirus!! A Record of the Battle!! めたん Nov 16, 2026 21:09 Table of Contents Sudden Onset The Result of the Hospital Visit About a Day After Onset Thinking About the Cause *I have written about my specific infection control measures in the next article, so please take a look if you need them. Sudden Onset It was a Sunday, just into November. After finishing my plans for the day, we were having dinner and relaxing with some drinks... when suddenly my husband started saying he felt cold. He quickly began to look unwell, and around midnight, the vomiting started. My husband's vomiting continued all night, every 30 minutes to an hour, and as someone with emetophobia, I was teary-eyed. At first, I thought maybe he had just had too much to drink, but by 2:00 AM, I started to suspect food poisoning or gastroenteritis. Around 4:00 AM, my husband said, "Maybe I'll feel better if I take some stomach medicine," so I went to a 24-hour drugstore to buy stomach medicine, oral rehydration solution, and other supplies. However, by that time, he was already in a state where he would vomit even after drinking water. In the end, when morning came, I had to urgently ask my sister-in-law, who lives nearby, to take him to the hospital and stay with him, while I headed to work having barely slept. The Result of the Hospital Visit In a LINE message from my sister-in-law around noon, it said he was diagnosed with viral acute gastroenteritis. The doctor said that since they didn't perform a test, they couldn't identify the specific virus, but given the symptoms, it was likely norovirus, which left me in despair. However, this made our course of action clear. During a break at work, I bought some hypochlorous acid water spray and headed home. About a Day After Onset When I got home, my husband was exhausted and sleeping, but fortunately, the vomiting had stopped. He was also able to sip oral rehydration solution and Pocari Sweat. Since he hadn't been able to keep any fluids down at all the previous night, I felt a little relieved. And so began the intense disinfection work...! I used the hypochlorous acid spray to disinfect not only the toilet bowl but also the walls, floor, doorknobs, and flush lever, and I thoroughly disinfected every place in the room he might have touched. What was difficult was disinfecting the clothes he had been wearing. They didn't look dirty, but I thought it would be dangerous if droplets had landed on them, so I decided to disinfect them. However, they weren't white, so I couldn't use bleach. I decided to disinfect them with boiling water, but my husband is a large man, and his clothes were bulky, so it was quite a struggle. I boiled water in two large pots and also used a kettle to finish the disinfection, but afterward, I started to worry if I had actually managed to keep the temperature above 85°C... Also, it turned out that the towel he used to wipe his mouth when he vomited had been brought into the bed without being washed. I ended up having to disinfect the towel and the bed as well, and I was completely worn out. Since we are adults, we managed to get through it, but it really made me realize how truly difficult it would be if a child caught norovirus. In the end, my husband's vomiting and diarrhea stopped on Tuesday morning, and he was able to return to work the following week. Perhaps thanks to the disinfection, I have spent the days since without any symptoms. Thinking About the Cause After he was diagnosed with what was likely norovirus, I thought many times about where he could have picked up the virus. The only thing that comes to mind is the restroom at the shopping mall. We went to the mall on Saturday, had lunch, and only my husband went to the restroom. I think he probably picked up the virus there. Since the incubation period for norovirus is generally said to be 24 to 48 hours, it makes sense if he was infected on Saturday afternoon and the symptoms started on Sunday night. What I realized from this experience is the danger of restrooms used by the general public. Since many different people use them, there are naturally some who are unwell, and I learned that it is very possible to get infected from doorknobs or other surfaces by using the same restroom. What I want to be careful about is handwashing. Even if the virus gets on your hands via a doorknob, it wouldn't be a problem if you wash your hands thoroughly at the end to rinse the virus away. This experience really made me realize how important it is to wash your hands properly. Handwashing and gargling are the basics of disease prevention. Moderna's $4 Billion Spending Plan Meets a Norovirus Setback and a Legal Chief's Exit - ad-hoc-news.de

Moderna's $4 Billion Spending Plan Meets a Norovirus Setback and a Legal Chief's Exit - ad-hoc-news.de
新闻简报Moderna's $4 billion spending plan for 2027 faces a setback as its norovirus vaccine trial did not meet an early-success threshold, according to a news report published on 11/15/2026. The Phase 3 study, which involved approximately 294,000 U.S. veterans aged 65 and older, failed to meet the criteria for early success, prompting the company to state it will not add another cohort this season. This development contrasts with Moderna's raised revenue growth guidance for 2026, which was increased to 15%-25%. The company's financial commitments are highlighted as a key focus, but the clinical outcomes of its vaccine programs remain uncertain. The report also notes the retirement of Moderna's legal chief, with a severance payment of $1,628,300, and the ongoing search for a successor. The article emphasizes that while financial forecasts are clear, the success of research investments is still pending. The stock price dropped 3.2% following the report, reflecting investor concerns over the limited visibility of clinical results.

时间
发布于 2026 年 11 月 15 日,事件日期与 Moderna 的 诺如病毒 疫苗 的第 3 期研究相关,未达到早期成功阈值。
地点与对象
Moderna 的 诺如病毒 疫苗 的第 3 期研究涉及大约 294,000 名 65 岁及以上的匹配美国退伍军人。
事件
Moderna的诺如病毒 疫苗试验没有达到早期成功门槛,该公司表示本赛季不会再增加队列研究。2026 年 11 月 15 日发表的一篇新闻文章对此进行了报道。
规模、影响与风险
The norovirus vaccine trial did not meet the early-success threshold, indicating potential clinical uncertainty. No specific case or death counts were reported for the norovirus vaccine, but the company's financial forecast for 2027 was raised to $4 billion in cash costs.
应对、状态与不确定性
Moderna 没有提供有关 诺如病毒 疫苗 性能的更多详细信息,只是表示它没有达到早期成功的阈值。该公司正在继续寻找其法律负责人的继任者,并且尚未披露 疫苗 计划的其他步骤。

Modernas, Billion Moderna's $4 Billion Spending Plan Meets a Norovirus Setback and a Legal Chief's Exit Published on 11/15/2026 at 09:10 | Editorial boerse-global.de Moderna lifted 2026 revenue growth guidance to 15%-25% and set 2027 cash costs near $4 billion, while its norovirus vaccine missed an early-success threshold. Moderna's $4 Billion Spending Plan Meets a Norovirus Setback and a Legal Chief's Exit Illustration mit AI erstellt. Moderna has given investors plenty to weigh this week: a retirement agreement for its top lawyer, a raised revenue outlook, a multi-year spending framework, and a norovirus trial that fell short of an early-success threshold. The common thread is a familiar one for the vaccine maker — promised spending on one side, unproven clinical payoff on the other. Discover more Track Biotech Find Vaccines Vaccine A Departure With a Price Tag Shannon Thyme Klinger, Moderna's legal chief, intends to retire, according to a regulatory filing published yesterday. She will stay on through the transition until March 31, 2027, while the company searches for a successor. Under the terms of the agreement, disclosed in the SEC filing, she is due a severance payment of $1,628,300. The figure is precise and contractual. The returns on Moderna's research are neither. That contrast is the lens through which the week's disclosures are best read: committed obligations can be counted to the dollar, while the economic value of pipeline bets has yet to be proven. Costs Laid Out, Forecast Withheld At Thursday's analyst day, Moderna guided to cash costs of roughly $4 billion for 2027. For the 2027–2030 stretch, it put the annual figure at about $4 billion to $4.5 billion, with oncology investment named as the main driver. No formal financial forecast for 2027 was offered — management said that will come alongside fourth-quarter results. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying Moderna? Media reports pinned the subsequent share-price weakness on investor disappointment over that limited visibility. The stock closed Friday down 3.2%. A cost framework describes intended outlays; it does not answer the question of the overall financial result. Spending discipline, in other words, should not be confused with keeping the research budget as small as possible. What matters is the progress those outlays buy. Growth Guidance Rises On the commercial side, Moderna lifted its 2026 revenue growth expectation to 15%–25%, up from prior guidance of growth of up to 10%. For the third quarter of 2026, it reported revenue of $1.3 billion and GAAP net income of $0.2 billion. Discover more Check Symptoms Soothe Digestion Study Biology That profitable quarter is an important counterweight to the spending debate. Still, it would be just as reductive to read it as proof that the research investments are paying off as it would be to treat every dollar of spending purely as a burden. Norovirus Miss Tempers the Optimism Vaccine development supplied a reminder of the limits of confidence. The Phase 3 study of Moderna's norovirus vaccine did not meet the criteria for early success, and the company said it will not add another cohort this season. Moderna at a turning point? This analysis reveals what investors need to know now. That is not evidence of final failure. It does show why a brighter revenue forecast does not dissolve clinical uncertainty — both statements belong to the same company and have to coexist. Veteran Data, With Caveats Among other disclosures were comparative data for the mNEXSPIKE vaccine. The Moderna-supported observational study covered roughly 294,000 matched U.S. veterans aged 65 and older. According to the company, mNEXSPIKE was associated with a 35.9% lower risk of medically treated COVID-19 compared with Comirnaty, and the reported difference for COVID-19-related hospitalization was 47.0%. These are associations from an observational study, not results from a randomized comparison. For Moderna, the yardstick is not a single trading session but the relationship between money committed and progress demonstrated. Growth buys room to maneuver. Whether that turns into durable economic success depends on what the research delivers. Ad Moderna Stock: New Analysis - 15 November Fresh Moderna information released. UKHSA Issues Urgent 48-Hour Stay-Home Warning as Norovirus Rises - JournalArta
新闻简报英国健康安全局 (UKHSA) 已向出现 诺如病毒 症状的个人发出紧急 48 小时居家警告,建议他们避免在学校、工作场所和医院与其他人密切接触,直到症状消退两天后。该通报基于已证实的数据,显示 诺如病毒 病例有所增加,本季节有 2,111 份实验室报告,比同期五个季节的平均水平高出 16.8%。UKHSA 指出,感染病例已开始增加,截至上周的两周内已报告 257 例,但总体数字仍处于预期水平内。该机构强调,诺如病毒 具有高度传染性,洗手液无法有效防止其传播。建议的措施包括用肥皂和水洗手、清洁受污染的表面以及在症状期间避免与他人接触。UKHSA 正在积极监测局势,并根据当前数据发出警告,除了报告的病例上升之外,没有任何迹象表明出现更广泛的 暴发。
查看新闻五要素
时间
UKHSA 于 2026 年 11 月 14 日发出警告,据报道 诺如病毒 病例在整个季节持续增加。
地点与对象
该警告适用于英格兰,针对学校、工作场所和医院中可能接触 诺如病毒 的个人。
事件
英国健康安全局 (UKHSA) 对出现 诺如病毒 症状的人发出紧急 48 小时居家警告,建议他们在症状消退两天前避免与他人密切接触。这是经过证实的公共卫生建议。
规模、影响与风险
本赛季诺如病毒的实验室报告已达到2111份,比同期五赛季平均水平高出16.8%。截至上周,感染病例已开始增加,两周内已报告 257 例。UKHSA 表示,该数字仍处于预期水平内,但病例的增加表明在密切接触环境中 传播 的风险不断增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
UKHSA 已确认该建议并提供了预防指南,包括洗手和清洁表面。该机构正在积极监测情况并根据当前数据发出警告。

UKHSA Issues Urgent 48-Hour Stay-Home Warning as Norovirus Rises - JournalArta

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) has issued an urgent 48-hour stay-at-home warning for individuals with norovirus symptoms, advising them to avoid close contact with others in schools, workplaces, and hospitals until two days after symptoms subside. This advisory is based on confirmed data showing a rise in norovirus cases, with 2,111 laboratory reports this season, 16.8% above the five-season average for the same period. The UKHSA notes that the infection has begun to increase, with 257 reports over the two weeks up to last week, but the overall figure remains within expected levels. The agency emphasizes that norovirus is highly infectious and that hand sanitizers are ineffective in preventing its spread. Recommended measures include washing hands with soap and water, cleaning contaminated surfaces, and avoiding contact with others during the symptomatic period. The UKHSA is actively monitoring the situation and issuing warnings based on current data, with no indication of a broader outbreak beyond the reported rise in cases.
Original source text (English)

People with symptoms should stay home until 48 hours after they stop, and hand sanitiser will not kill the virus, the agency warned. By Alistair Sterling November 14, 2026·2 min read UKHSA Issues Urgent 48-Hour Stay-Home Warning as Norovirus Rises The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) has issued an urgent 48-hour stay-at-home warning as norovirus cases rise in England, telling anyone with symptoms to keep away from school, work and hospitals until two days after they stop. Study Biology Posting on X, the agency said there have been 2,111 laboratory reports of norovirus so far this season. That is 16.8% above the five-season average for the same period. The figure remains within expected levels, according to the UKHSA. But the agency said the infection has begun to increase, with 257 reports over the two weeks up to last week. Study Biology What the advice says Norovirus is often called the winter vomiting bug, although people can catch it at any time of year. It causes vomiting and diarrhoea and spreads easily where people are in close contact, including hospitals, care homes, cruise ships, schools and nurseries. Advertisement The UKHSA previously said: "Norovirus is incredibly infectious, so if you're feeling unwell stay home for 48 hours after symptoms stop as you are still infectious during this time." It also warned that hand sanitiser will not work to prevent the illness. People can catch the virus more than once in a short period. Several genotypes, or types, circulate at the same time, and immunity to one strain does not stop infection by another. Study Biology Steps the NHS recommends The NHS says alcohol hand gels do not kill norovirus. It advises washing hands with soap and water after using the toilet or changing nappies, and before preparing, serving or eating food. Other NHS steps include: Advertisement Washing clothes and bedding soiled with faeces or vomit at 60C, separately from other laundry. Cleaning toilet seats, flush handles, taps and bathroom door handles. Avoiding contact with others as much as possible. Anyone with symptoms should not go to school, nursery or work until they have gone two days without being sick or having diarrhoea. They should also avoid visiting hospitals or care homes for the same period. The NHS says symptoms can appear suddenly and usually include nausea, vomiting and diarrhoea. It describes the illness as "very unpleasant, but usually gets better in about 2 days." Soothe Digestion Most cases can be treated at home, the NHS says, with plenty of fluids to avoid dehydration. People usually start to feel better in two to three days. Anyone with diarrhoea for more than seven days, or a child or adult vomiting for more than two days, should call 111. 诺如病毒占仁川食物中毒病原体的57%...冬季需注意 - 아시아경제

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报仁川市健康环境研究所的新闻报道显示,2026年1月至9月仁川地区疑似食物中毒病例中检测到的病原体中,诺如病毒占57.2%。报告强调了该病毒在冬季的患病率、其在低温下的生存能力,以及通过受污染的食物、水或与感染者接触而容易感染传播。该研究所敦促居民保持个人卫生并采取预防措施,以遏制诺如病毒的传播。它还强调避免为有症状的人准备食物并彻底清洁炊具的重要性。这些发现基于官方数据,是追踪感染源和实施风险因素调查的持续努力的一部分。该报告作为公共卫生警报,敦促在病毒可能迅速传播的群体环境中保持谨慎。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告发布于2026年11月13日。数据指2026年1月至9月疑似食物中毒病例。
地点与对象
韩国仁川广域市。受影响的人群包括托儿中心和学校等群体环境中的居民和个人。
事件
仁川市健康环境研究所报告称,2026年1月至9月疑似食物中毒病例中检出的病原体中,诺如病毒占57.2%。这是根据该研究所的数据确认的结果。
规模、影响与风险
311起疑似食物中毒案件中,诺如病毒负责178起,影响较大。该病毒很容易传播,是冬季食物中毒的主要原因,在群体环境中存在潜在风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究所强调需要采取个人卫生和预防措施,以减少 诺如病毒 的传播。他们正在开展食物中毒危险因素调查和感染源追踪调查。

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
Norovirus Accounts for 57% of Food Poisoning Pathogens in Incheon... Caution Needed in Winter - 아시아경제
News Brief
A news report from the Incheon Metropolitan City Institute of Health and Environment reveals that norovirus accounted for 57.2% of pathogens detected in suspected food poisoning cases in the Incheon area from January to September 2026. The report highlights the virus's prevalence during winter, its ability to survive in low temperatures, and its ease of transmission through contaminated food, water, or contact with infected individuals. The institute urged residents to maintain personal hygiene and follow preventive measures to curb the spread of norovirus. It also emphasized the importance of avoiding food preparation for those with symptoms and thoroughly cleaning cooking utensils. The findings are based on official data and are part of ongoing efforts to trace infection sources and implement risk factor surveys. The report serves as a public health alert, urging caution in group settings where the virus can spread rapidly.
Original source text (Korean)

Caution Needed in Winter Language Language Close KR CN Open Search bar Search field News Search Remove icon Search icon Dim Society Region Norovirus Accounts for 57% of Food Poisoning Pathogens in Incheon... Caution Needed in Winter by Park Haesook Published 13 Nov.2026 09:48(KST) Share Language Text Size Print The Incheon Metropolitan City Institute of Health and Environment urged residents to maintain personal hygiene and follow preventive measures to prevent food poisoning caused by norovirus infections during the winter. According to the institute on November 13, norovirus accounted for the largest share of pathogens detected in suspected food poisoning cases in the Incheon area from January to September this year, with 178 of the 311 cases (57.2%). Norovirus is a major cause of food poisoning in winter. Because it can survive even at low temperatures, infections tend to increase each year from November through the following spring. Even a small amount of the virus can cause infection, and it spreads easily, so extra caution is needed in group settings such as childcare centers and schools. The main routes of infection include consuming contaminated food or water, contact with an infected person, and contact with environments contaminated by vomit or feces. After an incubation period of 12 to 48 hours, infected people may develop symptoms such as vomiting, diarrhea, and abdominal pain. Norovirus food poisoning prevention guidelines. Provided by the Korea Disease Control and Prevention Agency. View original image To prevent norovirus food poisoning, people should follow basic preventive measures: wash their hands with soap under running water for at least 30 seconds; thoroughly cook shellfish, including oysters, at a core temperature of at least 85°C for at least 1 minute; boil water before drinking it; and thoroughly clean and disinfect cooking utensils and surrounding areas. In particular, anyone with suspected symptoms such as vomiting or diarrhea should avoid preparing food and should not take part in food preparation for at least 48 hours after their symptoms have subsided. 首尔市开始强化预防感染和救治能力 - 매일경제

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报针对诺如病毒疫情,首尔市政府宣布了加强托儿所和幼儿园感染预防和管理的计划。政府将于 2026 年 11 月 19 日在西小门政府大楼举行研讨会,传染病专家和现场工作人员将分享有关 诺如病毒 传播、消毒方法和应对策略的见解。据韩国疾病管理本部称,今年诺如病毒患者数量与去年同期相比增加了32%,报告病例数为10,575例。即使在夏季,检测案件也上升至814起,比上年增加约2.2倍。0至6岁的婴幼儿占感染人群的一半以上,凸显了采取有针对性的预防措施的必要性。政府的目标是根据研讨会制作教育材料并将其分发给日托中心和幼儿园,以改善感染控制措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
首尔市政府于2026年11月13日宣布召开研讨会,定于2026年11月19日举行。诺如病毒疫情预计从11月开始增加,一直持续到次年1月(N5)。
地点与对象
该活动在韩国首尔举行,具体地点是西小门政府大楼。受影响的人群包括日托中心和幼儿园,重点是0至6岁的婴幼儿,在去年样本监测的患者中,此类人群占到了二分之一以上(N6)。
事件
在诺如病毒疫情爆发之前,首尔市正在组织召开研讨会,以加强托儿所和幼儿园的感染预防和管理。研讨会将讨论传播的主要因素、正确的消毒方法以及实际反应案例(N8、N9)。
规模、影响与风险
据韩国疾病管理本部统计,今年诺如病毒患者人数为10,575人,比去年同期(N3)增加了32%。即使在夏季,诺如病毒 检测病例数也为 814 例,比去年同期(N4)增加约 2.2 倍。
应对、状态与不确定性
首尔市政府计划将研讨会的主要演讲制作为视频教育材料,并发布在其网站上。他们还打算向日托中心和幼儿园(N14、N15)分发预防和管理指南。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Korean original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Korean)
The Seoul Metropolitan Government has started to strengthen its capacity to prevent infection and re.. - 매일경제
News Brief
The Seoul Metropolitan Government has announced plans to enhance infection prevention and management in daycare centers and kindergartens in anticipation of the norovirus epidemic. The government will hold a symposium on November 19, 2026, at the Seosomun Government Complex, where infectious disease experts and field workers will share insights on norovirus transmission, disinfection methods, and response strategies. According to the Korea Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, the number of norovirus patients this year has increased by 32% compared to the same period last year, with 10,575 cases reported. Even in the summer, detection cases rose to 814, about 2.2 times higher than the previous year. Infants and toddlers aged 0 to 6 make up more than half of the infected population, highlighting the need for targeted prevention measures. The government aims to produce educational materials from the symposium and distribute them to daycare centers and kindergartens to improve infection control practices.
Original source text (Korean)
